Callback bodies carry no X-Reference-Id, so the caller passes the reference from the callback URL path (or we rely on `externalId` = reference). Callback endpoint hosting is a later ticket (#78/#114 consumers). Unrecognised provider status stays pending (never assumed final). Fake sandbox rejects non-EUR like the real sandbox is documented to.

| Criterion / edge case | Test / evidence |
|---|---|
| Path filters so only changed packages build | `PathFilterBehaviour` (admin-only change builds only admin; docs-only; flutter_shared builds both apps; one app only; function change runs core+appwrite; shared files/workflows run everything; unknown/missing base fails safe; real `git diff`), `CriterionPathFilters` (every package job filtered by `changes`; every implemented package has a job or recorded exemption (two `spikes/` with reason); job commands and working-directory equal `packages.json`; new package without job is reported; every package path triggers a group) |
| Single required check `ci-ok`, skipped jobs ok | `CiOkAggregate` (needs all jobs, `always()`, fails on failure/cancelled, not on skipped, 3 negative tests) |
| Staging deploy automatic on main | `DeployWorkflows.test_staging_triggers_on_push_to_main_only`, staging jobs use environment + flag. REAL run: owner-blocked (needs DEPLOY_ENABLED, Fly, Appwrite staging) |
| Production deploy requires manual approval | `test_production_is_manual_only_and_uses_production_environment`, `test_production_verifies_sha_is_on_main`. Required reviewers on the environment: OWNER setting |
| Nothing deploys until enabled | `test_every_deploy_or_secret_step_is_guarded_by_the_flag`, `test_job_without_flag_or_environment_is_reported`, `test_unguarded_deploy_step_is_reported` |
| Failed deploy does not leave staging half-migrated / migration fails halfway: no promotion | `MigrationFailureAndOrder` (API jobs need schema and gate on result AND `promote == 'true'`; runner contract `GITHUB_OUTPUT promote=`; `test_runner_failure_end_to_end...` runs the real runner with a failing migration: non-zero exit and no `promote=true`; negative tests for ungated/unordered jobs) + docs/ops/deploy.md (expand-only rule keeps the old API working on a partly advanced schema) |
| Schema and API deploy order; rollback plan documented | `test_staging_api_jobs_need_schema...`, `test_production_single_job_orders_schema_before_api...`, `test_deploy_doc_states_order_rollback_and_expand_contract`, `docs/ops/deploy.md` (order, expand/contract, backward compatible for one release, rollback table: API = redeploy previous image) |
| Two schema runs never overlap (F02 reviewer note 3) | `test_schema_runs_never_overlap` (group `appwrite-schema-<env>`, cancel false), `test_deploy_workflows_never_cancel`, `test_schema_job_without_own_concurrency_group_is_reported` |
| F02 note 1 (runner traceback prints no PROMOTE=false) | gate uses exit code AND promote output; end-to-end runner test; the runner fix itself remains an F02 follow-up |
| Security: no pull_request_target, minimal permissions, fork PRs get no secrets, pinned actions | `SecurityRules` (incl. negatives for secret in PR workflow, write-all, missing permissions, tag-pinned action, persisted credentials, untrusted `github.event.*` in `run`) |
| HS_STRICT, concurrency with cancel on PRs, caching | `CriterionPerformanceRules` |
| Workflow commands match documented commands | `CriterionPathFilters.test_job_commands_match_packages_json` (+ wrong-directory negative) |
| Dependabot for pip(uv)/npm/pub/actions | `test_dependabot_covers_every_ecosystem_and_lockfile`, gap negative test |
| Mobile builds to Play internal / TestFlight | OWNER-BLOCKED. Skeleton workflow (dispatch-only, flag-guarded) + `docs/ops/mobile-builds.md` (Codemagic vs Fastlane, exact owner steps); `test_mobile_*` |
| Trivial PR green; merge updates staging within 15 min | Green: the PR's own checks (reviewer reads). Staging within 15 min: OWNER-BLOCKED, unmeasured |
| YAML is really valid | `test_workflow_files_are_readable_by_the_real_yaml_parser` (my reader vs PyYAML through `uv run --with pyyaml`; skips offline), actionlint clean |

### X01 · Travel-time service with zone-pair caching (#85)

- **Status:** blocked-foundation-merged. PR #188 squash-merged (issue #85 closed). Owner steps: Google Cloud project, restricted server key, quota cap, budget alerts, real-route calibration of the fallback, apply migration 0007 to a non-live project.
- **Reviewer (round 1, 25 Nov 2026):** rebased onto main with A01 (3 trivial conflicts: schedule.py, settings.py, .env.example; union, generated files regenerated), pushed. core pytest 978 passed + ruff clean; appwrite unittest 393 OK; schema_build --check 69 tables; secrets_check ok, --write no diff; scripts tests 189 OK; make lint 11/0; make test 13/0; pre-commit passed; PR ci-ok green. Mutations failed as expected: single-flight, post-claim re-check, plausibility, Kampala-day reset, Retry-After cap. Probes: 50 threads one pair = 1 call, TTL-expiry stampede = 1, N x 1 zone = 1 call, first-ever implausible answer = flagged haversine + 1h negative entry, Dec31/Nov30 rollovers ok, alerts once per threshold, schema validator mutations fail.
- **Reviewer follow-ups (non-blocking, for a builder before W01 uses the service):** (1) `session_minutes` ignores `provider.billable`: with no key it consumes budget, writes usage rows and fires a false `daily_budget_exhausted` alert; (2) Google quota exhaustion is HTTP 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED: map to ProviderQuotaExceeded (30 min, no retry), now treated as rate limit (5 min, 3 attempts); (3) multi-instance overshoot is not bounded by 'one batch': lost updates undercount (measured 3 guards x 17 concurrent batches on a shared slow store: 300 granted vs cap 100; single process exact); reword docs/schema note, GCP quota cap is the hard stop; (4) set `follow_redirects=False` explicitly in the adapter client with a test, and document env-proxy (trust_env) behaviour; (5) no circuit breaker for `session_minutes` during outages (3 attempts + backoff each call); (6) job polls cancel between bands, not between provider chunks; (7) add `travel-cache-refresh` failure and travel alert routes to ops/alerts.json (F08); NaN/inf values for TRAVEL_FALLBACK_* pass validation.
- **Built:** `services/core/src/hs_core/travel/`: `geo.py` (integer micro-degree validation incl. null island, Uganda bbox warning, exact degree strings for the provider, haversine, fallback formula + band speed factors), `bands.py` (morning_peak/off_peak/evening_peak/night in Africa/Kampala, night crosses midnight, weekends no peaks, future weekday departure for the provider), `provider.py` (`TravelTimeProvider`, `Point`, `Element`, error classes, scriptable `FakeTravelTimeProvider`, `GoogleRoutesProvider` = Routes `computeRouteMatrix` adapter written from public docs, mock-tested only, UNVERIFIED), `cache.py` (`TravelCacheStore`, in-memory + Repository-backed; one row per origin/dest/band; last good value kept, failures only set `retry_at`/`failure_reason`/`fail_count`), `budget.py` (`BudgetGuard`: elements per Kampala day and month in `travel_usage`, 80%/100% monthly alerts, daily exhausted alert, warming share), `alerts.py` (hook + fake + log sink), `metrics.py`, `service.py` (`TravelTimeService.minutes`, `minutes_many`, `session_minutes`, `warm_origin`, `needs_session_lookup`), `jobs.py` (`travel-cache-refresh`), `factory.py` (no key = free straight-line only). - **Deviations / notes:** (1) Per-session close-call lookups are provided as an API (`session_minutes`, `needs_session_lookup`); the matching engine (W01/E09) decides when to call them. (2) `estimated=false` for same-zone (a configured default, independent of Maps). (3) Budget counters are read-modify-write (Appwrite has no atomic increment): multi-instance overshoot of about one batch; documented. (4) Fallback is pessimistic for far zones (Kololo-Entebbe about 144 min vs roughly 60-90 real); calibrate (owner step 7). (5) Zone rows of retired zones stay in the cache (harmless; retention job may purge). (6) Alerts go to a log sink (ERROR, `alert=travel-*`); not added to `ops/alerts.json` (F08 owns NFR-17 alert definitions): follow-up to add routes. (7) Google adapter element limit (100), TRAFFIC_AWARE and the error shapes are from docs, unverified. (8) `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` inventory row already existed (F09); no change.
- **Follow-ups:** matching tickets call `minutes_many` (N teacher zones x child zone) and `session_minutes` for close calls; add `travel-cache-refresh` failure/ budget alerts to `ops/alerts.json`; admin screen for `travel_usage` (M06/D-tickets); retention purge of old `travel_usage` rows.

### A01 · Phone OTP sign-up and sign-in, shared Flutter package (#43)

- **Status:** **blocked-foundation-merged** (PR #187, squash commit e47b595; issue #43 closed). Owner-blocked: Africa's Talking account, sender ID, real SMS, real-device tests (see Blocked on owner).
- **Design (Appwrite has no Africa's Talking provider):** the core service owns the OTP. `POST /v1/auth/otp/request {phone, app}` and `POST /v1/auth/otp/verify {phone, code, app, device}` (unauthenticated, `Cache-Control: no-store`). Request: normalise to E.164 (+256 mobile, 9 digits starting 7; local normaliser, nothing imported from py_momo), per-number resend cooldown (30 s, from the issue; the brief said 60 s: configurable), caps per number (5/h, 10/day) and per IP (20/h, 60/day), 6-digit `secrets.randbelow` code, only `HMAC-SHA256(OTP_PEPPER, challenge|salt|code)` stored, SMS through `SmsSender`; a failed send rolls everything back (no cooldown, quota or attempts burnt). Verify: per-IP cap, per-number failure cap (10 wrong/h across all codes = `otp_locked`), latest challenge only, one constant-time compare on every path, expiry (`now >= expires_at` is expired), 5 attempt slots, single-use consume claim, then find-or-create Appwrite user by phone, `user_profile` row (id = auth user id, `read("user:<id>")`), label + `role_assignment` history row, and `users.createToken` (120 s) returned as `{user_id, secret, expire, is_new_user, dormant}`; the app calls `account.createSession(userId, secret)`.
- **Concurrency idiom (ADR 0002/F04):** no conditional update in Appwrite, so every counter is slot rows with IDs derived from (owner, n); `create` 409 = `Conflict` = slot taken. Attempts: `att:<challenge>` slots 1..5. Single use: one `consume` row with ID derived from the challenge; of N simultaneous verifies exactly one creates it. Rate limits: `bucket` slots per window (`req:ph:h:<hash>:<hour>` etc.). If the Appwrite side fails after the consume claim, the consume row is deleted so the user's code is not burnt by our outage.
- **Roles, not by client claim alone:** `app` selects only between the two self-service labels `parent` and `teacher` (anything else is `invalid_app`, no privileged label is reachable), it is bound to the challenge at request time and must match at verify, existing labels are kept, a blocked user gets 403 only after proving the number. `teacher` label = access to the teacher app's own screens; approval/data access come later (VER-1, ADR 0012 per-user grants). Same number in both apps = one user with two labels.
- **Schema:** `appwrite/schema/auth.json` (new domain, table `otp_challenge`, server-only, hashes only: no phone, code or IP; hard delete) + `appwrite/migrations/0006_auth_schema.py`. **Migration number:** 0003 sessions (M02, merged), 0004 money (M04) and geo (X01), 0005 catalogue (M03) are taken by branches in flight, so 0006 at rebase time; renumber if the order changes (the file name is the only thing to change, plus the test `test_migration_file_exists_and_is_numbered_after_core` only needs > 2). `docs/erd/auth.md` and `appwrite.config.json` regenerated. Also changed `appwrite/tests/test_schema.py` (`applied[:2]`, domain lookup by name: same edit M04 makes) because `auth` sorts before `core`.
- **Job:** `otp-cleanup` hourly in `jobs/schedule.py` (`auth_otp/jobs.py`): deletes rows expired more than 1 h ago in chunks of 40 (`Continue`), idempotent, NotFound ignored. F04 handlers get no dependencies, so the repository is bound at startup (`jobs.bind`, module-level): F04 follow-up for a proper DI.
- **Secrets/config (F09):** new Settings `AFRICASTALKING_API_KEY` (secret), `AFRICASTALKING_USERNAME` (`sandbox` selects the sandbox endpoint), `AFRICASTALKING_SENDER_ID`, `OTP_PEPPER` (secret, required and >= 32 chars outside dev: existing deployed-settings tests got `OTP_PEPPER` added), `OTP_APP_HASH`, and the limits `OTP_TTL_S, OTP_MAX_ATTEMPTS, OTP_RESEND_COOLDOWN_S, OTP_PHONE_REQUESTS_PER_HOUR/_DAY, OTP_IP_REQUESTS_PER_HOUR/_DAY, OTP_PHONE_FAILURES_PER_HOUR, OTP_IP_VERIFIES_PER_HOUR, OTP_DORMANT_DAYS` (range-checked at startup). All in `.env.example` and `docs/ops/secrets-inventory.json` (new procedure `otp-pepper`; `python3 scripts/secrets_check.py --write` run), `OTP_PEPPER` declared first-party in `docs/legal/processors.json` + register. App dart-define `HS_CORE_URL` (public, https outside dev) added to `AppConfig` and the app READMEs (apps' `HS_*` defines are not in the inventory, as before).
- **Logging/privacy:** the service logs the masked number (`+256 *** *** 456`) and events only; F08 scrubber extended: `_OTP_TEXT` (code/otp/pin/passcode followed by 4-8 digits, linear-time tested at 250 KB) and exact keys `otp, otpcode, salt, codehash, smsbody, verificationcode, passcode`. Over-masks `error code 5000`-style text (documented in the regex comment). Errors from the SMS adapter never contain the key, code or number.
- **SMS:** `auth_otp/sms.py`: `SmsSender`, `FakeSmsSender`, `UnconfiguredSmsSender` (503 `sms_unavailable`, also the dev default), `AfricasTalkingSender` (httpx, mock transport only, endpoints/headers/status codes marked **unverified until a real account is used**). Body in `sms_otp.en.txt`; with `OTP_APP_HASH` set it uses the Android SMS Retriever layout (`<#>` prefix, hash last line), otherwise plain.
- **Flutter (`packages/flutter_shared`, package `home_school_shared`):** `AuthService` gains `requestOtp/verifyOtp/refreshSession` (fake covers wrong code with attempts budget, expired, too many attempts, rate limited, locked, offline, SMS outage, latency); `CoreOtpApi` (maps the server error codes), `HttpApiClient` (dart:io), `OtpFlowController` (Riverpod `NotifierProvider`, countdown timer, double-submit guard), `PhoneEntryScreen`/`CodeEntryScreen` (+256 fixed, 3-3-3 formatter, 6 boxes over one real field with `oneTimeCode` autofill, auto-submit on 6th digit, masked number, error live regions, help sheet), routes `/sign-in/phone` and `/sign-in/code`, Welcome gets "I already have an account", `SessionVault` over `flutter_secure_storage` (no phone number or one-time token stored), `AppwriteAuthService` rewired behind a `SessionGateway` (testable), `SessionRefresher` (extends the session on resume). Strings in the ARB (`flutter gen-l10n` run). Apps: `main.dart` builds `CoreOtpApi` with the app's `AppRole` (parent/teacher), restores/extends the session before `runApp`, and starts on the first tab when signed in (`initialLocation`). `apps/*/pubspec.lock` gained the secure-storage packages. Test files in `apps/*` updated: Get started now opens phone entry.
- **Tests / results:** `cd services/core && uv run pytest -q` -> 801 passed (231 new in `tests/test_otp_*.py` + 20 scrubber cases in `test_otp_logging.py`; ruff check/format clean); `cd appwrite && python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` Ran 216 OK (`test_schema_auth.py`); `docs/legal` 39 OK + check-processors ok; `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests` Ran 189 OK; `cd packages/flutter_shared && flutter test` 167 passed, apps/parent 6, apps/teacher 6; `flutter analyze` and `dart format --set-exit-if-changed .` clean in all three; `make setup && make lint` 11 ran ok 0 failed; `make test` 13 ran ok 0 failed.

| Criterion / edge case | Test |
|---|---|
| +256 fixed, validated for Ugandan formats | `test_otp_phone.py` (12 valid + 20 invalid shared fixture cases, non-strings, 1 MB input); Dart `phone_test.dart` (same fixture, formatter); `otp_flow_test.dart` "fixed +256, note...", "formats as +256 772 123 456...", "invalid number: message, nothing sent..." |
| 6-digit code, auto-submit on 6th digit | `test_otp_service.py::test_code_is_six_digits_and_sent_to_the_normalised_number`, `test_codes_come_from_a_csprng_and_keep_leading_zeros`; `otp_flow_test.dart` "six boxes... correct code signs in on the 6th digit", "only digits are accepted, at most six", "Verify button ... disabled until six digits" |

Own attacks (in-process app, fakes): all 1,000,000 codes against one challenge (per-IP cap neutralised) = 5 evaluated, 0 successes; slot races (200 threads, also with injected latency) give exactly `limit` winners, unique IDs; 12/50/200 simultaneous correct verifies = 1 token, 1 user, 1 profile; 299.9 s ok, 300.1 s expired; old code after a newer challenge, other phone, switched `app`, reuse all refused; phone variants share one identity and one limit, unicode/trailing/other-country inputs rejected; bad `app` values (opsadmin, superadmin, curator, ops_admin, arrays, objects, case) rejected with no rows written; known vs unknown numbers identical in status, body, headers and timing (2000 interleaved samples; no user-directory call on request or wrong verify); no code/number/token in logs or rows; SMS or Appwrite outage leave no state and do not burn the code; staging/production refuse missing, blank, placeholder or <32 char `OTP_PEPPER`; XFF ignored, `Fly-Client-IP` trusted outside dev, garbage header falls back to the socket peer; pepper rotation only affects in-flight codes and resets counters (nothing long-lived is keyed by it); mutated phone fixture fails both Python and Dart. Teacher label alone gives no data access (ADR 0012 per-user grants, VER-1 approval); recycled number: the old account's per-user rows would be readable, flagged only (owner question 25).
- **Reviewer advisories (non-blocking, follow-ups):** (a) a staff-labelled user (e.g. `opsadmin`) with a phone on the Appwrite account gets a normal session through the mobile flow (and the `parent`/`teacher` label), without the admin web MFA step: refuse or step up phone sign-in for privileged labels (new owner question 27, default: refuse generic error after the code is proven); (b) per-IP caps are per address, so an IPv6 /64 can rotate: bucket on /64; (c) the 30 s resend cooldown can be raced (5 simultaneous requests all sent, bounded by the 5/h cap); (d) the per-number failure window is a fixed clock hour (up to 20 wrong codes across a boundary); (e) `AppwriteUserDirectory.add_label` is read-modify-write (concurrent sign-ins or an admin label change can lose a label); (f) the code field has `enableInteractiveSelection: false`, so no paste menu (keyboard/SMS autofill works); (g) `.env.example` lists integer settings with empty values (same pre-existing pattern as `JOB_ALERT_AFTER_FAILURES=`): copying it verbatim to `.env` fails int parsing.
- **Deviations:** (1) resend cooldown 30 s (issue AC) not 60 s (brief): `OTP_RESEND_COOLDOWN_S`. (2) Android SMS Retriever (read the SMS silently) is not integrated; the field carries `AutofillHints.oneTimeCode` and the SMS has the hash layout when `OTP_APP_HASH` is set. (3) `Session.secret` is only stored when Appwrite returns it to the client (unverified), otherwise the SDK cookie is used and only identifiers are stored. (4) Routes are not guarded (a signed-out user can open `/home`); a redirect on session loss belongs with the first feature screens. (5) Dev without provider credentials has no way to receive a code (503 by design; tests use the fake): use the AT sandbox once an account exists. (6) `trust_proxy_ip` = outside dev the `Fly-Client-IP` header is trusted (Fly sets it at its edge): unverified on a real Fly app. (7) InMemoryRepository does not emulate unique indexes, so the profile-phone 409 is simulated by patching `create`.
- **Follow-ups:** job-handler DI (F04); schema `user_profile.phone` unique index vs soft delete (M01 follow-up 1) will block re-signup of a deleted account's number; phone change flow (authenticated, OTP to the new number: Account settings, SR "change flow with OTP"); step-up verification for payout-number changes on dormant accounts; Luganda strings; Play Integrity / App Attest if abuse appears; session-expiry redirect; `claim_slot` costs a list plus up to limit creates (fine at pilot scale).

### $01 · Double-entry ledger service (#76)

- **Status:** **changes-requested** (reviewer round 1; branch `s01-ledger` 97be29d).
- **Reviewer round 1 (commands, all on a fresh worktree of 97be29d):** `uv sync && uv run pytest -q` 1257 passed (ledger tests 279 x3 runs: 279/279/279), ruff check/format clean, `secrets_check.py` ok, `schema_build.py --check` ok (69 tables), `make setup/lint/test` 11 ok 2 skipped 0 failed / 13 ok 0 failed, `unittest discover -s scripts/tests` 189 OK, `uvx pre-commit run --all-files` all passed. Own repros: crash harness over 2/3/4-line transactions, crash before/after every write x second crash during the retry x 2 clocks (224 scenarios): no half post ever visible in balance/trial_balance/statement, retry converges to 1 header + n lines, audit once; orphans with other amounts/accounts/line count -> `IdempotencyConflict`; amount fuzz (0, -1, 1.0, True, Decimal, 2**63, 10**30, nan, inf, '100', None, bytes) rejected on both stores; single/empty/duplicate/unknown/same-account-both-sides rejected; reverse twice (same reason) idempotent, reversing a reversal refused; 200 threads, 40 keys, 3 readers with jitter on every repo call: 0 violations, balances exact. Mutations killed by the suite: no balance check, no seal, readers count unsealed (totals and entries), no amount validation, `differences` always empty, `_verify_line` no-op, posted_at reuse removed, ignore currency, memory-store content check removed, duplicate lines allowed, reversal mirror unchecked. Survivor: removing the "orphan has extra line numbers" check (equivalent mutant: a balanced 2-4 line request cannot share a line prefix with a longer balanced one; defence in depth only). 20,000 entries: `balance` 0.13 s, `trial_balance` 0.18 s in the fake (fake time says nothing about Appwrite latency: ~111 list calls for one 10,000-entry account at ~300 ms each is ~30 s; see finding 2).
- **FINDING 1 (blocking, money integrity): two concurrent workers on the SAME key with different clocks can write lines with DIFFERENT `posted_at`, and the header with a third.** `appwrite_store.append` reads the orphans once (`existing`), then each worker writes with its own `tx.posted_at`; a worker that loses the create race on line k only runs `_verify_line` (account/side/amount/transaction) and CONTINUES writing the remaining lines and the header with its own timestamp. The "reuse the first attempt's posted_at" rule only covers sequential retries. Repro (300 runs, two services over one `PagingRepository`, clocks 10 s apart, random 0-2 ms sleeps around every repository call, 4-line adjustment `Dr cash_mtn 100, Dr cash_airtel 50, Cr revenue 90, Cr receivable 60`): 57/300 end with entries at 09:00:00.002 and 09:00:10.001 (header one of them). Then `balance(code, as_of=09:00:00.002)` = cash_mtn 100, cash_airtel 50, revenue 0, receivable -60: a half-posted transaction is visible in `balance`/`statement(as_of)` while `trial_balance(as_of)` (which filters on the HEADER time) shows all or nothing; also line `posted_at` != header `posted_at`. Without `as_of` nothing is wrong, so impact is limited to as_of reports/period close/reconciliation windows, but it breaks the stated invariant (no half-posted transaction visible in balance/statement) on a money ledger and no test catches it (the concurrency tests use one shared clock). **Fix:** make line 1 the single source of `posted_at`: write/verify line 1 first, then re-read the persisted line 1 and use ITS `posted_at` for every other line AND the header (also after a 409 on line 1); `_verify_line` must compare `posted_at` too (and `reference_type`/`reference_id`/`currency`, see finding 3), and `_seal` replay should compare header `posted_at` with line 1. Add a test: two services with clocks 10 s apart, random sleeps/barrier between repository calls (or a deterministic gate that makes both read zero orphans before either writes), assert all lines and the header share one `posted_at` and `balance(as_of)` at any instant between the two clock values shows all or none; run it on both stores' contract if applicable.
- **Finding 2 (non-blocking, document):** (a) cost on real Appwrite: `balance` is O(entries of the account) with one header lookup per 100 entries, ~111 list calls at 10,000 entries, minutes at 100k: state in `docs/ops/ledger.md` the point where a roll-up is needed (suggest: per-account entries > ~5,000, or any balance read on a request path above that), and that `trial_balance` reads every header and every entry (not for request paths). (b) `statement` is in repository creation order, not `posted_at` order, and not stable across stores (the memory store orders by transaction insertion); a recovered orphan (older `posted_at`, later creation) appears out of place. Document, or sort by (posted_at, transaction_id, line) in the consumer. (c) no alert/metric for abandoned groups beyond a `ledger.orphan_abandoned` warning log; no F04 job entry point for `recover()` yet (recorded as follow-up; keep it on the list for $05/R04: an abandoned group means a payment the ledger does not know about).
- **Finding 3 (non-blocking, fix with finding 1):** retrying an orphaned key with the same lines but different `reference_type`/`reference_id`/`description`/`kind` is ACCEPTED and sealed with the second request's header while the orphan lines keep the first's `reference_*` (repro: lines `x/r1`, header `y/r9`); `reconcile_payments` reads line `reference_*`. `_verify_line` should compare reference_type/reference_id/currency (the header-only fields cannot be checked: say so in the docs). Also: `reverse()` twice with a DIFFERENT reason raises `IdempotencyConflict` (same reason replays): fine, document it. Partial reversal is not supported (a reversal must mirror exactly); say so in `docs/ops/ledger.md`.
- **Finding 4 (non-blocking, record precisely):** the docs follow-up for the stale "nothing uses it yet / not wired" wording is recorded only as "an M04 doc test". Exact places to change together: `services/core/src/hs_core/append_only.py` docstring (line 4), `services/core/tests/test_append_only.py:171` (asserts "nothing uses it yet" in README and doc), `appwrite/tests/test_schema_money.py:283-290` (asserts "not wired yet" in the ledger table notes), `appwrite/schema/money.json` notes for `ledger_transaction`/`ledger_entry` (and the generated `appwrite/README.md`/`docs/erd`, regenerate with `schema_build.py --write`). `docs/ops/ledger.md` heading "What it guarantees" should say the guarantees hold for writers that use the service/wrapper; the wrapper is opt-in and the server API key can still update/delete rows (the Appwrite-vs-Postgres table already says it; repeat it at the top).
- Verified OK: accounts/sides per flow (invoice Dr receivable Cr revenue; payment Dr cash Cr receivable; earning Dr revenue Cr teacher payable; payout, refund/credit as normal-side-debit `revenue` contra, fee) give a balanced trial balance; contra accounts reported by normal side, no sign error; keys `<prefix>:<source id>` do not collide across flows (the prefix is part of the key and the id; nothing stops a caller using `payment:` with kind adjustment: acceptable, $02 should use `payment_received`); ledger writes only via `AppendOnlyRepository` (create/get/list), `recover()` never deletes, no offset/`total` use (grep), page 1,000 (<= 5,000); no stored running totals; no new Settings; diff is $01 + docs only; hash collision of line ids between different transactions is cryptographically negligible (sha256 truncated to 128 bits) and a 409 there is caught by `_verify_line` (`transaction_id` compared) anyway. Audit: `AuditSink.record(AuditEvent(key, action, entity_type, entity_id, actor_id, detail))` is adaptable to M06's `AuditService.record(actor, action, entity, entity_id, before, after, reason, ...)` with a thin adapter (actor_id -> actor, entity_type -> entity, detail -> after, reversal_reason -> reason), provided M06 accepts a caller-chosen deterministic row id for `ledger:<tx id>`; no M06 branch existed to compare against. Accrual semantics and `invoice`/optional key/kind/roll-up schema follow-ups are recorded (owner questions already listed). Owner-blocked items (Q10, accountant, staging, real reconciliation) acceptable.
- After the fix, re-submit; reviewer will re-run the suite plus the 300-run two-worker repro and open the PR (expect `blocked-foundation-merged`).

- **Built (`services/core/src/hs_core/ledger/`, Python API only, no HTTP, no new Settings):** `money.py` (`Money`, `check_amount`: plain int 1..10^12; floats/bool/str/Decimal refused), `model.py` (ids, value objects, `differences`), `chart.py` (7 fixed SR 7.10 accounts + `teacher_payable:<id>`), `store.py` (**`LedgerStore` Protocol**, the swap boundary), `appwrite_store.py` (write protocol below, through `AppendOnlyRepository`), `memory.py` (reference store), `service.py` (`LedgerService`: `post`, `reverse`, `balance`, `trial_balance`, `statement`, `ensure_chart_of_accounts`, `ensure_teacher_payable`, `recover`), `flows.py` (pure helpers: invoice, payment per provider, earning, payout, refund, credit, fee, adjustment, `reversal_of`; key convention `<prefix>:<source id>`), `reconcile.py` (`reconcile_payments`, `reconcile_cash` for $05), `audit.py` (`AuditSink` for M06, deterministic key `ledger:<tx id>`, called on every post incl. replays), `testing.py` (`PagingRepository` = Appwrite-like fake with default limit 25, cap 5000, cursorAfter, unique indexes; `FaultyRepository` crash before/after the Nth write; `FakeAuditSink`). Docs: `docs/ops/ledger.md` (protocol, swap boundary, Appwrite vs Postgres trade-offs, performance limits, schema follow-ups); `services/core/README.md` section.
- **Write protocol:** deterministic ids (hash of key / (tx, line) / code) so `create` 409 = already written; lines 1..n first, header (`ledger_transaction`) LAST = seal (the schema's own rule); readers count only entries whose header exists; trial balance takes the sealed set first, then sums only those, so it is one consistent snapshot; retry finds orphans, verifies them (else `IdempotencyConflict`), reuses the first attempt's `posted_at`, writes the rest, seals. `recover(rebuild=)` completes stale orphans only when the caller can rebuild the request (lines carry no key/kind), else reports `abandoned` (invisible forever; a later retry still completes). One reversal per original: key `reversal:<id>`, lines must mirror, a reversal cannot be reversed.
- **Tests:** `cd services/core && uv sync && uv run pytest -q` -> 1257 passed on the rebased tree (core was 598 at M04; ledger ~280); ruff check/format clean; `python3 scripts/secrets_check.py` ok; `make setup && make lint` 11 ok 2 skipped 0 failed; `make test` 13 ok 0 failed. Honest process note: the first test file was written and seen red (collection error) before the service; the store/service code for the later test files (crash, concurrency, scale, fuzz, flows, mutations) was written ahead of those tests, with the mutation tests as compensating evidence (8 mutations + 1 idempotency-removal, each makes its check fail; the last one is also stopped by the unique index).

- **Deviations / notes:** (1) account types follow the M04 enum (no `contra`): refunds+credits is `revenue` with normal side `debit` (`is_contra`); (2) the M04 `kind` enum has no `invoice`: invoices are `adjustment` with reference_type `invoice`; (3) schema has no roll-up table, so no stored period totals: balances page by cursor (document: O(entries of the account)); (4) no `content_hash`/key on lines, so orphan completion needs the caller's `rebuild`; (5) the accrual semantics (invoice -> revenue, earning -> Dr revenue / Cr teacher payable) are my reading of SR 7.10; (6) M04 docs say "nothing uses AppendOnlyRepository yet / not wired": now stale (ledger uses it) but pinned by an M04 doc test, left for a docs follow-up; (7) `ensure_chart_of_accounts` / `recover` are not scheduled anywhere: the app startup and F04 job wiring belong to the tickets that post money.

### B03 · Subscription lifecycle and billing periods (#71)
- Status: **ready-for-review** (commit 97b6cea, rebase before PR). Branch `b03-subscription-lifecycle` (commit starts `B03:`, rebased on main, pushed with --force-with-lease, PR not opened). Foundation: real payments, ledger ($01), audit (M06), notifications (N01/N03) and sessions (S01) are behind interfaces with fakes; expect `blocked-foundation-merged` only if the reviewer treats the unwired parts as owner-blocked, otherwise `merged` once the wiring follow-ups below are tracked.
- **Built** (`services/core/src/hs_core/billing/`): `states.py` (pure transition table `TRANSITIONS` + `transition()`; every move returns the `EventName`s it implies, nothing is sent; forbidden pairs raise `ForbiddenTransition`), `periods.py` (monthly periods on Kampala dates stored as UTC instants; anchor-based month-end clamping, no drift; `sessions_included`), `models.py`, `interfaces.py` (`BillingLedger`, `AuditSink`, `Notifier`, `SessionScheduler` with `cancel_future`/`regenerate`, `PlanCatalog`, `DiscountPolicy`), `fakes.py`, `store.py` (Repository-backed; Conflict-based claims), `service.py` (`BillingService`), `jobs.py` (`billing-period-roll`, registered in `jobs/schedule.py`, daily 03:00 Kampala, chunk 20, keyset cursor, error-isolated, honours `ctx.cancelled`; unbound until wired: reports "not configured"). Settings `BILLING_RENEWAL_LEAD_DAYS` (5), `BILLING_GRACE_DAYS` (3, SR section 8), `BILLING_PAST_DUE_CANCEL_DAYS` (14), `BILLING_MAX_PAUSE_DAYS` (30), `BILLING_ROLL_CHUNK_SIZE` (20) in `Settings` (range-checked), `services/core/.env.example`, F09 inventory (`secrets_check.py --write`, 82 entries).
- **Design decisions (evidence in brackets; defaults are owner questions, see below):**
  - Transition table: pending_payment -payment_confirmed-> matching -teacher_assigned-> active; active <-pause/resume-> paused; active -payment_overdue-> past_due -payment_confirmed-> active; past_due -grace_expired-> cancelled; `cancel` from every non-terminal status; cancelled terminal. Self loops with events: payment_confirmed on matching/active/paused (renewal), teacher_assigned on active (rematch). Not added: active -> matching for a trial rematch (X07 owns trial outcomes; owner/X07 to say if the subscription should go back to matching).
  - Concurrency: Appwrite has no conditional update, so a transition first CREATES claim row `subscription_transition(sub, version+1)` (Conflict = lost: `ConcurrentTransition`), then updates the subscription, then marks the claim applied. A claim whose writer died is rolled forward after a 30 s lease (`repair`), including its events/audit. Residual risk (documented in `store.py`): `repair` is read-then-write.
  - Period: anchor = Kampala date of subscription creation (SR 7.8 "invoice created at period start"; issue "payment arrives after period start: period start stays"). Period k starts on the anchor day-of-month of month+k, clamped (31 Jan, 28 Feb, 31 Mar). Invoice `period_start_date`/`period_end_date` are Kampala dates (end inclusive); `period_start_at`/`period_end_at` UTC instants of Kampala midnight; `due_at` = period start (first invoice: creation time).
  - Sessions included (rule, SUB-3): `sessions_per_week * days_in_period // 7` integer, rounded down (2/week = 8 in 28/30/31 days; 3/week = 13 in 31 days). Alternatives for the owner: flat 4 weeks, or actual weekday occurrences from the schedule (S01).
  - Credits (SUB-5): `record_platform_miss(sub, session, reason)` one credit per session; a payment after the period start credits `sessions_per_week * lost_days // 7` (lost days = from the period start, or from the day the subscription went past_due, to the payment date). Credits apply to the NEXT invoice at generation: value per credit = `price // sessions_included` (integer floor), whole credits only, never below zero, leftovers carry over; each applied credit is posted through `BillingLedger.record_credit_applied`.
  - Pause: immediate, `cancel_future(reason="paused")`, the paid period is NOT extended or refunded, no renewal invoices while paused, resume inside the paid period keeps it (`regenerate`), resume after it re-anchors on the resume date with a new invoice; limit predicate `pause_limit_exceeded` (B04/ops call it).
  - Cancel: active -> `cancel_at_period_end` (sessions continue; the roll job cancels at the boundary and calls `cancel_future`); pending_payment, matching, paused, past_due, or `immediate=True` (admin) cancel now. Unpaid invoices are voided; a paid first period cancelled in `matching` is put on the refund queue (`flag_refund_due`, reason `cancelled_before_activation`). `keep` undoes cancel-at-period-end. Cancelling twice is a no-op.
  - Plan change (SUB-4): pending until the next period's invoice; NO proration, no refund, no top-up (default, owner question). If the next invoice already exists the change applies one period later.
  - Grace/past due (PAY-3, SR 7.8, B04 owns reminders and pausing sessions): `mark_past_due` (active -> past_due only when an unpaid invoice is `grace_days` past its due date, else `GraceNotExpired`) and `expire_past_due` (past_due -> cancelled after `past_due_cancel_days`) are the hooks; no job calls them yet.
  - Trial: `is_trial` and `assignment_id` are recorded on the subscription from `on_teacher_assigned`; billing is unchanged by it (the first period is paid before matching, PRD flow 1).
  - Payments: `on_payment_confirmed(invoice_id, payment_ref, amount_ugx=None)`: one application per provider reference (`billing_payment` claim; duplicates return `duplicate`, concurrent ones `in_progress`, a crashed one is resumed after a 120 s lease), one "paid" claim per invoice (a second, different payment is `flagged` `duplicate_payment` and put on the refund queue), wrong amount -> `amount_mismatch` flag, cancelled subscription or void invoice -> `late_payment_after_cancellation` flag; none of the flagged cases advance state. `on_payment_status(invoice, ref, status)` only acts on `succeeded/successful/confirmed`; pending/unknown (`OutcomeUnknown`)/failed/expired never change anything. The same payment reference for another invoice raises `PaymentRefReuse`.
  - Dispatch: after the commit the service calls `Notifier.notify` and `SessionScheduler.cancel_future`/`regenerate` with deterministic event ids (`<sub>:<version>:<name>`); a failing consumer never undoes the commit (counted in `dispatch_failures`). Audit through `AuditSink` for every transition and payment, ids and statuses only (no PII in logs; test `test_logs_carry_ids_only...`).
- **Tests:** `cd services/core && uv sync && uv run pytest -q` -> 1131 passed (incl. ~150 new B03 tests in `test_billing_states.py`, `test_billing_periods.py`, `test_billing_service.py`, `test_billing_job_and_config.py`); ruff check and format clean. States/periods tests were seen failing first (collection error); the service tests were written against the fixed API before the service, then made green. Race tests (50 threads: double tap, same payment x50, two payments for one invoice x50, pause vs cancel x50, roll job x50) repeated 6x without failure. Property tests (seeded random sequences): no forbidden state, invoices unique per (subscription, period start), amounts are ints <= price, version == applied claims, cancelled absorbing; period tiling and Kampala/UTC boundaries over 3 years of days.
- **Mutation checks (each fails the suite, reverted; script not committed):** skip payment idempotency; allow forbidden transition; float credit unit; float sessions included; UTC date instead of Kampala; invoice id not unique per period; month-end drift (clamp to 28); no optimistic claim; late payment after cancel applied; duplicate payment for one invoice not flagged; pending status advances state; period start moves to payment date; credit applied twice; credit overdraws invoice; double tap creates a second subscription; renewal ignores cancel flag; crash-resume check removed; grace ignored. One survivor (credit re-assignment) found and a test added.

- **Wiring / schema follow-ups (needed before this runs for real):**
  1. **Schema (new migration, owner/M-ticket; not done here to avoid migration-number collisions with parallel builders):** new tables `subscription_transition` (append-only history/claims; unique id by (subscription, to_version)), `billing_credit`, `billing_payment`, `billing_invoice_paid`, `billing_child_live`; extra `subscription` columns `version`, `anchor_date`, `period_index`, `cancel_at_period_end`, `pending_plan_id`, `past_due_at`, `first_payment_at`, `last_payment_ref`, `assignment_id`, `is_trial`, `created_at`; extra `invoice` columns `plan_id`, `period_index`, `plan_price_ugx`, `discount_ugx`, `credit_ugx`, `credit_ids`, `payment_ref`. Pinned by `test_every_stored_column_is_in_the_schema_or_in_the_pinned_follow_up_list` (the test fails when the schema gains them, forcing the list to be updated). The live `invoice` period start/end are varchar(10) dates (M04); the invoice unique index `(subscription_id, period_start_date)` is the DB backstop for the derived invoice id.
  2. **Ledger ($01):** implement `BillingLedger` (`record_payment`, `record_credit_applied`, `flag_refund_due`; idempotent by key) over the real ledger, and a `payment` row writer: this ticket does not create `payment` rows (the MoMo collection flow, #78/#114, owns them) and only consumes `on_payment_confirmed`.
  3. **Audit (M06), Notifier (N01/N03), Scheduler (S01):** implement the interfaces and call `billing.jobs.bind(BillingService(...))` plus `main.create_app` wiring (not wired: nothing binds a service, so the job is a no-op). S01 must make `cancel_future`/`regenerate` idempotent by `event_id`. An outbox/redelivery sweep over `subscription_transition` claims (events are not re-sent if the process dies between commit and dispatch, except for repaired claims) is a follow-up.
  4. **B04/$04:** call `mark_past_due` and `expire_past_due` from the reminder job; pause sessions for past_due subscriptions; B04 also calls `pause_limit_exceeded`.
  5. **X07:** trial outcome flows (rematch while `active`?), see the table note.
  6. **Plan table / admin:** `RepositoryPlans` reads `plan`; plan price changes affect only invoices generated afterwards.
- **Questions for the owner (defaults in brackets; all coded as settings or documented policy):** (B03-1) renewal invoice lead time [5 days]; (B03-2) grace period [3 days, SR section 8] and the past_due -> cancelled limit [14 days]; (B03-3) plan change/cancel proration [none: plan change from the next period, cancel at the end of the paid period, no refund]; (B03-4) pause: limit [30 days], whether a pause should extend the period or pause billing [no: paid period is not extended, renewals are skipped while paused]; (B03-5) sessions included rule [floor(sessions/week x days / 7); alternatives: flat 4 weeks, or the real weekday count]; (B03-6) credits: should a payment after the period start (or past_due days) credit sessions at all when the delay is the parent's [yes, per the issue], and the credit value per session [price // sessions included, floored]; (B03-7) cancel of an `active` subscription [end of paid period; admin may force immediate]; cancel in `matching` after a paid first period [immediate + refund flagged]; (B03-8) whether a trial rematch moves `active` back to `matching` [not allowed today, X07].

### S01 · Generate recurring sessions from assignments (#93)
- Status: **ready-for-review**. Branch `s01-session-generation` (commit starts `S01:`, rebased on main, pushed with --force-with-lease, PR not opened). Blockers #37 (M02) and #30 (F04) are merged. Owner-blocked only for applying migration 0008 to a real project and the questions below (defaults implemented).
- **Built (all under `services/core/src/hs_core/scheduling/`):** `rules.py` (PORT of `appwrite/session_rules.py` + Kampala helpers), `pattern.py` (preferred slots -> occurrences), `leases.py` (row-based per-teacher lease), `writer.py` (lease + overlap check + insert + re-check-after-insert rollback; create / reschedule / soft_delete / restore), `generator.py` (`SessionGenerator`: `generate`, `regenerate(assignment_id, from_date)`, `cancel_future(assignment_id, from_date, reason)`, `generate_now`, `run_chunk`), `issues.py` (`scheduling_issue` store), `audit.py` (`AuditSink` interface: M06 supplies the real one), `job.py` (`session-generate`), `api.py` (`POST /admin/scheduling/generate-now`, opsadmin). Wiring: `jobs/schedule.py` (job: daily 01:00 Kampala, `catch_up` 1 day, max 3 attempts), `main.create_app(scheduler=...)`, `settings.py` (4 settings), `repository.py` (`cursorAfter` support in the in-memory fake, additive). Schema: `appwrite/schema/sessions.json` (session + `slot_ref`, `generation_key` unique, `is_trial`, `overridden`), NEW domain `appwrite/schema/scheduling.json` (`teacher_schedule_lease`, `scheduling_issue`), migration `appwrite/migrations/0008_session_generation.py` (renumber if another schema ticket took 0008; X01 uses 0007), regenerated `appwrite.config.json` and `docs/erd/{sessions,scheduling}.md`. Config: `.env.example` + F09 inventory rows (`SESSION_GEN_HORIZON_DAYS` 28, `SESSION_GEN_LEASE_TTL_S` 30, `SESSION_GEN_LOCK_WAIT_S` 3, `SESSION_GEN_CHUNK_BUDGET_S` 6; ranges validated at startup), `docs/ops/config-matrix.md` regenerated.
- **Design decisions (read these first):**
  1. **Port, not import, `session_rules.py`.** `appwrite/` is not a core dependency (Docker image) and a top-level `appwrite` dir would shadow the Appwrite SDK. `tests/test_sched_rules.py` loads the reference file by path and proves equivalence (state tables, every pair of states, overlap/time validation on random intervals, key builders and patches, `soft_restore_patch`). Documented in `rules.py` and both READMEs.
  2. **Identity of a generated session** = `slot_ref` (`<ISO weekday>-<start minute>:<Kampala local date>`, never changes) and the unique derived `generation_key` = `<assignment_id>:<slot_ref>`. It stays on cancelled/void/no_show/rescheduled rows (so they are never regenerated or recreated at the old time) and is nulled ONLY by soft delete (`cancel_future`, race rollback), so a paused slot can be regenerated. `soft_delete_session_patch` nulls both keys; `restore_session_patch` recomputes both (issue note: never a bare `deleted_at: None`; the 409 test is `test_soft_delete_releases_both_keys_and_restore_recomputes_them`). The literal-`None` lint of M02 is satisfied via a named constant `rules.CLEARED` inside the restore helper (the only place).
  3. **Per-teacher serialisation (closes the M02 residual risk).** Layer 1 lease: row per (teacher, generation); creating generation N+1 (deterministic row id + unique `lease_key`) is the atomic claim (409 = busy); a holder is valid while its generation is the highest AND not expired (fencing); `release` updates only its own row; holders prune rows older than the previous generation. Layer 2 check: bounded read of the teacher's sessions (index `by_teacher_day`) before insert. Layer 3 backstops: unique `teacher_slot_key` and `generation_key` (a 409 is classified by re-reading: same key -> `AlreadyGenerated`, else `SessionConflict`). Layer 4 re-check after insert: if an overlapping slot-holding session ranks before ours (`$createdAt`, then `$id`) ours is soft-deleted and `SessionConflict(raced=True)` raised. Chains A~B~C may roll back one row too many: safe, self-heals next run. Reschedule goes through the same path (`writer.reschedule`, marks `overridden`, reverts on a lost race).
  4. **What happens on a teacher leave day** (issue #93 edge case "substitute requests created at generation"): the session row is created already `cancelled_by_teacher` (slot released), plus an immutable `cancellation` row (`by_role=teacher`, reason `teacher_leave`, `is_late=false`) and an OPEN `match_request` type `substitute` (deterministic ids, so idempotent; order cancellation -> match_request -> status update for sessions that already existed, crash-repaired by the next run). Leave logged AFTER generation is reconciled by every nightly run. Substitute requests are therefore opened as early as generation (up to 28 days ahead): owner question 2.
  5. **Conflicts are reported, never overwritten:** `scheduling_issue` rows (`teacher_conflict`, `outside_availability`, `holiday`, `no_home_location`, `no_slots`, `slot_shortfall`, `invalid_slot`, `teacher_inactive`, `generation_error`), unique `issue_key` while open (re-running never duplicates; a standing conflict costs reads only), auto-resolved when the condition clears. Admin queue UI = M06/W-tickets.
  6. **Quota:** `sessions_per_week` of the plan selects the first N preferred slots by (weekday, start); fewer slots than the plan -> `slot_shortfall` issue. Nightly `generate` is create-only (plus leave/availability reconcile); `regenerate(from_date)` also prunes surplus future SCHEDULED generated sessions (never overridden/cancelled/started/substitute rows, and anything starting at/after `assignment.end_at`). `cancel_future` soft-deletes future scheduled generated rows from a date and closes open substitute requests of removed leave rows; substitute rows (`is_substitute`) are never touched.
  7. **Trial:** the first created session of an assignment with `is_trial` is flagged (the flag moves on if that slot could not be created, never two live trial sessions; a leave-cancelled row is not flagged).
  8. **Generation window:** from `max(today, from_date, assignment start)` (Kampala dates) to `today + horizon` (inclusive), clipped at `assignment.end_at`; sessions must start strictly after now. Only `subscription.status = active` generates. Teacher must be `active`. Home = parent's default `home_location` (or the only one). No clamp to `subscription.period_end_at` on purpose (billing renewals would otherwise leave a gap before payment).
  9. **Job:** `session-generate` pages active subscriptions with `cursorAfter`, `ctx.cancelled` polled between assignments, 6 s budget per chunk (always at least one subscription, so a zero budget still progresses), per-assignment error isolation (class name only, no message, into the alert and the issue), `AlertSink` hook `session_generation_failed`, a crash (`BaseException`) is not swallowed. A busy teacher defers that assignment to the next run.
  10. **Metrics:** structured logs, ids and counts only: `session generation` (assignment_id, teacher_id, status, reason, `rows_created`, existing, conflicts, flagged, leave_cancelled, pruned, resolved, `lag_days`, horizon_days, duration_ms) and `session generation chunk` (run_id, assignments, rows_created, conflicts, flagged, failures, deferred, skipped, max_lag_days). `lag_days` = horizon end minus the earliest date the run had to create (0 = only the edge was extended; a larger number after a normal first run means catch-up). Field name `sessions_created` is avoided: the log scrubber masks anything containing `session`.
- **Test commands and results:** `cd services/core && uv sync && uv run pytest -q` -> 1164 passed (after rebase onto main with X01/B03/M05 merged; 1032 before); `uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check .` clean; `cd appwrite && python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` -> Ran 410 OK; `python3 appwrite/schema_build.py --check` -> schema ok (69 tables); `python3 scripts/secrets_check.py` ok (71 entries); `make setup && make lint` -> 11 ran ok, 2 skipped, 0 failed; `make test` -> 13 ran ok, 0 skipped, 0 failed (before the final rebase; core/appwrite/lint re-run after it as above). Concurrency/property tests repeated: `uv run pytest -q tests/test_sched_writer.py tests/test_sched_property.py -k "fifty or thread or race or lease or mutation or takeover"` 15x + an in-process stress of the 50-thread race (150 runs, 0 rollbacks, 0 overlaps) and of the generator 50-thread tests (60 runs).
- **Honesty notes:** (a) the schema JSON was written before its test file was run (the test file existed first and failed on the missing schema; validator errors then renamed `generated_for` -> `slot_ref`, because `schema_build` treats names containing `rate` as money); the core tests were written first and seen failing on import. (b) The two single mutations "lease only removed" and "re-check only removed" do NOT fail the overlap invariant (defence in depth keeps it): they are detected by other assertions instead (no rollbacks may happen under the lease; the re-check tests; the stalled-holder tests). Removing BOTH fails (`test_mutation_without_lease_and_without_recheck_...`, writer and generator level). (c) `HookRepository`-style fake: the in-memory repository has no unique indexes, so `tests/sched_helpers.UniqueRepo` reads EVERY unique index from `appwrite/schema/*.json` and enforces them (NULLs never collide) plus a monotonic `$createdAt`. Real Appwrite behaviour of `cursorAfter`, `$createdAt` on create responses and 409 on unique violations is assumed from the docs, owner-blocked verification on a non-live project.

- **Deviations / notes for the reviewer:** (1) New schema domain `scheduling.json` instead of editing M02's sessions test expectations (`REQUIRED` table set in `test_sessions_schema.py`); the 4 new `session` columns do live in `sessions.json`. (2) `session` has no `is_trial` in SR 5.2/the original M02 schema: added here (assignment.is_trial marks the pairing, the session flag marks WHICH session is the trial). (3) Substitute requests for leave days are opened at generation time as the issue says; the matching ticket decides when to offer (owner question 2). (4) Teacher availability uses `availability_slot` only; a teacher with NO availability rows gets nothing generated and an `outside_availability` flag per slot (conservative; owner question 3). (5) `AssignmentNotFound`/`ReasonRequired` are plain exceptions mapped to 404/422 by the endpoint. (6) The admin endpoint is per assignment (not "all"): a full sweep belongs to the nightly job.
- **Questions for the owner (defaults implemented):** (1) Public holidays: SR does not say. Default: NOT auto-skipped; an optional calendar callable only flags them (`holiday` issue) and the admin decides. Needs a holiday list source if you want auto-skip. (2) Teacher leave: open the substitute request at generation (default, as the issue says, up to `SESSION_GEN_HORIZON_DAYS` ahead) or only N days before the session? (3) Teacher without availability rows or a slot outside availability: default = do not generate, flag for the admin; alternative = generate and flag. (4) Horizon length: default 28 days (`SESSION_GEN_HORIZON_DAYS`, range 7-90). (5) Plan change timing: B03 decides the `from_date` (SUB-4: next period); default regenerate leaves already-generated sessions of the old plan before `from_date` alone. (6) Should the first trial session be re-flagged when the trial session is cancelled and rescheduled by W-tickets (default: the flag stays on the row that carries the generation identity).
- **Follow-ups:** B03/B04 call `regenerate` / `cancel_future` (names and signatures fixed here); W01 #97 should use `SessionWriter.create/reschedule/soft_delete/restore` for every session write (it is the serialised path); M06 provides the real `AuditSink` (append to `audit_log` via the append-only wrapper) and the admin queue for `scheduling_issue`; M05 permissions for the two new tables (rows are written by the core service only; all have empty table permissions and row security); retention: `teacher_schedule_lease` is operational (hard delete, 1-2 rows per teacher), `scheduling_issue` is soft; the F04 reviewer follow-ups (per-job state isolation, `job_run` schema) still apply to this job; verify `cursorAfter` on a non-live project.

## Blocked on owner (credentials / deployments / accounts)

- F02: create the three Appwrite projects, keys and secrets-manager entries; run `appwrite push` and the runner against dev; staging push from CI (F07). Steps in the F02 ticket block.
- D02: staging demo of the job tick (needs deployed core service + non-live Appwrite project).
- D03: Fly.io account/org and billing; throwaway Fly app to measure Kampala to `fra`; mobile-network latency (MTN, Airtel) to Appwrite and Fly; a non-live Appwrite project (`hs-staging`, Frankfurt) with a scoped API key; Appwrite plan/cost confirmation; lawyer/DPO review of the DP-7 note (ADR 0012).
- D05: sending domain; Resend account (Free ok for staging); DNS access to add SPF/DKIM/MX/DMARC records for `mail.<domain>`; Resend API key + webhook secret into staging Fly secrets; DPA signed (DP-9); then the inbox test (Gmail, Yahoo, a Ugandan host). Exact steps: owner checklist in ADR 0005.
- F01: GitHub branch protection on `main` (Settings > Branches > rule for `main`): require a pull request before merging (1 approval can stay 0 because the reviewer agent posts comment reviews under the owner's account; enable "Dismiss stale approvals" off), require status checks to pass and branch up to date, the checks being the F07 (#33) job names (suggest `lint` and `test`, running `make lint` and `HS_STRICT=1 make test`), restrict force-push and deletion, and allow squash merge only. Optionally a CODEOWNERS review requirement and a GitHub team in `.github/CODEOWNERS` (currently `@Matt-Murungi`). Can only be applied once F07 CI exists.
- D06: engage a Ugandan lawyer; choose positioning; approve pilot registration route; do the PDPO registration, pay fee, sign DPAs; lawyer to deliver the written note and final policy texts.
- F03: Fly.io app `fly.toml`, staging deploy and secrets (`APPWRITE_API_KEY`, project/database IDs) per ADR 0003; non-live Appwrite project for staging; Docker image build/run proof (docker not installed on the build machine); AppwriteJwtVerifier and AppwriteRepository against a real non-live project.
- M01: apply `0002_core_schema` to a non-live dev/staging project (`python3 appwrite/migrate.py --env development`) and confirm the REST `ensure_table` shapes (column endpoints varchar/text/enum) once; live project is read-only.
- F06: staging Appwrite project + test admin with MFA to verify real login/MFA; deploy target and env values for the admin app (see F06 block).
- F05: iOS and release builds need Xcode/Codemagic, signing keys, and the app store accounts; app/bundle ids and brand name are undecided (placeholders `ug.homeschool.home_school_*`).

- F08: Sentry account + 5 projects and DSNs (Fly secret `SENTRY_DSN`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN`, dart-define); alert routing to owner email/push; uptime vendor account (Better Stack free, UptimeRobot free second check) + heartbeat URL; staging/production hostnames. Exact steps: `docs/ops/setup.md`. Defaults are free tiers, no paid plan chosen.
- F04: deploy `job-tick` to a non-live Appwrite project (F07 CI `appwrite push`), set function variables `CORE_SERVICE_URL`, `JOB_TICK_SECRET` and the same secret as a Fly secret; then watch `heartbeat` on staging every 5 minutes and fail one run on purpose. Check Appwrite plan allows ~43,000 executions/month.
- $02 (MoMo): (1) MTN MoMo developer account (momodeveloper.mtn.com); (2) subscribe separately to Collections and Disbursements (one subscription key each); (3) sandbox API user + API key per product (portal or `POST /v1_0/apiuser` + `/apikey`), stored in the secret store (F09), never git; (4) public HTTPS callback URL on the staging core service (endpoint built in a later ticket) and its host registered on the sandbox user; (5) run `packages/py_momo/scripts/manual_sandbox.py --yes-call-the-network` and report path/header/reason-code differences (README endpoints are unverified); (6) production certification R04 #152: production keys, real `X-Target-Environment`, real transaction limits.
- F07: all in `docs/ops/ci.md` "Owner checklist": branch protection on `main` requiring the single check `ci-ok` (+ up to date, no force-push, squash only); fork-PR approval policy and read-only default token; GitHub environments `staging` (branch `main`) and `production` (required reviewers = owner, branch `main`); repository variable `DEPLOY_ENABLED=true` (only when ready) and per-environment variables `APPWRITE_PROJECT_ID`, `FLY_APP`, `STAGING_CORE_URL`/`PRODUCTION_CORE_URL`; per-environment secrets `APPWRITE_API_KEY` (ci-deploy key) and `FLY_API_TOKEN` (`fly tokens create deploy`); Fly apps and Appwrite projects (F02/F03); admin host decision (ADR 0004 names none); mobile provider, store accounts, signing keys, bundle ids (`docs/ops/mobile-builds.md`); Dependabot enabled.

- X01 (travel time): Google Cloud project per environment with billing; enable Routes API; server key restricted to Routes (+ static Fly egress IP if used) stored as Fly secret `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY`; per-day element quota cap (recommended 1,500) and a billing budget with email alerts at 50/90/100% (recommended USD 50/month); apply `0007_geo_schema` to a non-live project; real-route sanity check of about 10 Kampala zone pairs at 17:30 to calibrate `TRAVEL_FALLBACK_SPEED_KMH`/`TRAVEL_FALLBACK_CIRCUITY`, confirm the adapter (element limit, error shapes) and the price per element. Exact steps and defaults: `docs/ops/travel-time.md`.
- A01 (phone OTP): (1) Africa's Talking account (Uganda), get the sandbox API key first (`AFRICASTALKING_USERNAME=sandbox`), then a live username and API key; top up airtime/balance (insufficient balance maps to a retriable "SMS unavailable"). (2) Register an alphanumeric sender ID (`AFRICASTALKING_SENDER_ID`, e.g. `HOMESCHOOL`) with Africa's Talking and the carriers (MTN, Airtel); lead time unknown; until then the provider's default sender is used and some carriers may filter it. (3) Generate `OTP_PEPPER` (`openssl rand -hex 32`) and set it with the AT values as Fly secrets per environment (`fly secrets import -a <fly-app>`), never in chat. (4) Apply `appwrite/migrations/0006_auth_schema.py` to a non-live project (`python3 appwrite/migrate.py --env development`). (5) With the sandbox, verify the unverified assumptions in `auth_otp/sms.py` (endpoint, `apiKey` header, form fields, recipient `statusCode` meanings) and in Appwrite: `users.list(search=<phone>)` finds by phone, `users.createToken(expire=<seconds>)`, `account.createSession(userId, secret)` accepts that token, `account.updateSession` extends the session, whether `Session.secret` reaches the client. (6) Real-device test on Android and iOS with an MTN and an Airtel number (issue "How we'll know"). (7) Compute the Android SMS Retriever hash from the release signing key and set `OTP_APP_HASH`. (8) Add `--dart-define=HS_CORE_URL=https://<core host>` to the Codemagic builds. (9) Tune the limits with real traffic (recommended starting values are the defaults: 5 codes/h and 10/day per number, 20/h and 60/day per IP, 10 wrong codes/h per number); MTN/Airtel CGNAT can put many users behind one IP, so watch `rate_limited` rates before tightening IP caps.

### M04 · Schema: money, tiers, safeguarding, audit, notifications (#39)
- Status: **merged** (PR #186, squash 9c95633; migration 0004 stays with M04; rebased onto M03 (0005) by the reviewer, generated files regenerated, 66 tables); history: was ready-for-review (round 3, commit 5cc3fb6); was ready-for-review (round 2, commit e5a9ca3); was changes-requested (reviewer round 1); was ready-for-review (round 1, rebased on main after M02 merged). Branch `m04-schema-money` (commit e340716, pushed with --force-with-lease; PR not opened). Blocked on owner only for applying anywhere real. (Re-done from scratch after the reboot wiped the first worktree.)
- **Built:** three domain files `appwrite/schema/{money,safeguarding,platform}.json` (12 + 3 + 5 = 20 tables): money: `tier, tier_history, bonus_rule, invoice, payment, earning, payout_batch, payout, ledger_account, ledger_transaction, ledger_entry, feedback`; safeguarding: `safeguarding_case, safeguarding_action, case_status` (reporter-visible status, ADR 0012); platform: `audit_log, notification, notification_template, notification_preference, device_token` (N01 #126 builds on these; it must NOT redefine them). Migration `appwrite/migrations/0003_money_schema.py` (applies the three domains through `schema_build.apply_domain`; **renumber to 0004 if M02's `0003_sessions_schema.py` merges first**: tests name `0003_money_schema.py`, update `Migration.test_money_migration_exists_*`). Generated `appwrite.config.json` and `docs/erd/{money,safeguarding,platform}.md` via `schema_build.py --write` (37 tables total). `services/core/src/hs_core/append_only.py` (`AppendOnlyRepository`: create/get/list, update/delete raise `AppendOnlyViolation`, other tables refused; `find_append_only_violations(root)` AST scan: update/delete/update_row/delete_row with an append-only table as literal or module constant). `appwrite/README.md` notes. Edited M01 test `test_schema.py` only to relax `applied == [1, 2]` to `applied[:2]` (a third migration now exists).
- **Decisions:** append-only set = `ledger_entry, ledger_transaction, audit_log, safeguarding_action` (marked `meta.append_only`, deletion `immutable`); a test checks the core constant equals the schema marks. Ledger: header `ledger_transaction` (idempotency_key UNIQUE, `reverses_transaction_id` UNIQUE so a posting is reversed once, entry_count, total_amount) plus lines `ledger_entry` (unique (transaction_id, line), `side` debit|credit, integer UGX `amount` >= 1, account FK). Balance invariants (sum debit == sum credit == total_amount, one currency) are documented core-service rules for $01 #76; NO `post()` validator here (the issue does not ask). `payment` and `payout` `idempotency_key` UNIQUE; also unique (provider, provider_reference), (batch_id, teacher_id), earning `dedupe_key`, invoice (subscription, period start), batch `active_key` (one live batch per period), feedback (session, parent). Provider is free text varchar (owner has not chosen providers). Safeguarding tables: empty table permissions, no label read on the table (per-row `read(user:<assigned lead>)` only, ADR 0012 scope rule); reporters read `case_status` only. `audit_log` read by per-row grants (opsadmin, lead). Money integer UGX, instants UTC (`*_at`), calendar dates varchar(10), FKs varchar(36) indexed; polymorphic ids declared `non_fk` with reason. FKs to M02's `session`/`subscription` are `deferred` relations (indexed); `core.json teacher_profile.tier_id` stays marked deferred (core.json not edited; the target now exists).
- **Soft delete vs unique (M02 rule applied; SOFT_UNIQUE_LEGACY untouched, still 6 M01 pairs):** (a) derived keys on tables that stay `soft`: `tier.active_key` (lower-case name while live), `feedback.active_key` (`session:parent` while live), `payout_batch.active_key` (period start while not cancelled); `soft_delete_patch` nulls them (test `SoftDeleteRule`). (b) Not soft-deletable (deletion `hard`, no `deleted_at`; lifecycle is the status column, only the retention job removes rows, after the retention period): `payment`, `payout` (idempotency keys and provider references must stay claimed forever, NFR-8), `invoice` (void is a status), `earning` (dedupe_key stays claimed), `ledger_account` (code never reused, retire with active=false), `case_status` and `notification_preference` (one row per case/user, removed with them under retention). Append-only tables have no `deleted_at` (immutable). Still soft, no unique: `bonus_rule`, `safeguarding_case`, `notification` (`dedupe_key` optional, `*_key`-named, so compliant).
- **Tests-first, honestly:** I wrote the schema JSON BEFORE its tests (only the core guard `append_only.py` was test-first: its tests were seen failing on import). The schema tests were then written against the schema, so I added reviewer-style mutation tests (`test_schema_money.ValidatorMutations`, each asserts `validate()` rejects the mutated schema and passes the unmutated one): undeclared FK, missing FK index, float money, `any` permission, table-level label read on the three safeguarding tables, raw unique on a soft table, soft-delete column on ledger_entry/audit_log/safeguarding_action, idempotency-key unique removed, (transaction_id, line) unique removed. Evidence that the new validator rule bites: before I applied it, `schema_build.py --write` after the rebase failed with 15 `soft-delete table ... unique ... must use optional derived *_key` errors on my own tables (tier, invoice, payment, earning, payout, ledger_account, feedback, notification_preference, case_status).

- Round 2 verdict (reviewer): **changes-requested (round 3, small)**. Verified at c65c9f2 in a fresh worktree: appwrite unittest 305 OK, `schema_build.py --check` ok (46 tables), `secrets_check.py` ok, `scripts/tests` 189 OK, `make setup/lint` ok (11 ran, 0 failed), `make test` 13 ran ok, `uvx pre-commit run --all-files` all Passed. Round-1 findings 1-4 confirmed fixed by repro: edit rejects subject/level/strand/parent of another or retired version, self parent, A->B->A, chain of 4, version_id change; happy edits work; s1 developing, s2 secure, resubmit s2 empty -> developing/s1; last evidence removed -> row deleted; 1,000 shuffles with equal instants (also mixed tz offsets) -> one result; handover gen keys `assignment:<id>:gen:<n>`/`session:<id>:gen:<n>`, index `(assignment_id, generation DESC)`, 50 concurrent first generators -> 1 row, 50 concurrent regenerators -> 2 rows total (gen 2 once); level_equivalence: self, same curriculum, retired, symmetric duplicate (B,A) all rejected, remove then re-add works; rating enum yes/partly/no, unique (summary, teacher) kept; mastery rule implemented = latest log wins (not secure-over-developing), consistent with #105 dated history; `SOFT_UNIQUE_LEGACY` still six; `session_log.child_id` has FK relation + index `by_child_submitted`; no snapshot content in diff. Two real defects remain (both small, fix + test, then re-review):
  1. **Soft-deleted references accepted, and copy-as-draft then crashes.** `check_topic_refs`/`check_strand_refs` check only the version, not liveness: `edit_catalogue_row`/`add_catalogue_row` on a topic with `subject_id`/`parent_topic_id` pointing at a soft-deleted subject/topic of the same draft succeed (repro: soft-delete a subject, add or edit a topic onto it). Also `soft_delete_catalogue_row` does not refuse when live children exist. Consequence: with a live topic under a soft-deleted subject, `publish_version` then `copy_version_as_draft` raises `KeyError` (copy skips the deleted subject but remaps the topic's subject_id through `ids`). Fix: reject non-live referenced rows in the ref checks, refuse (or cascade) soft delete of a subject/level/strand/topic that still has live dependants, and make copy robust; add tests.
  2. **`session_log.child_id` is unvalidated and can drift.** `submit_session_log` takes `child` from the caller, never checks it against the session's child (session -> assignment -> child), and on an existing log the patch never touches `child_id`. Repro: submit s9 for child ch1, resubmit s9 with a different child object: accepted, log keeps ch1, topics validated and progress recomputed for the OTHER child, ch1's progress stays stale. Fix: verify the session's child equals `child["$id"]` (and an existing log's child_id equals it), reject otherwise, add tests (wrong child on first submit, wrong child on resubmit).
  Non-blocking: issue #108 and #106 notes are now stale (#108 says `useful` boolean, #106 says key design unsettled): append a one-line update to each; a forged `$id` in an edit patch is passed to `update_row` (strip `$`-prefixed keys); `save_handover` with both assignment and session silently uses the assignment.

(4) `copy_version_as_draft` is not atomic if a write fails midway (leaves a draft with partial content; retry makes a new version). Superseded status line below.

- **Findings to fix (round 1), each with a failing repro:**
  1. **`edit_catalogue_row` skips the same-version check.** Edit of a draft topic with `{"subject_id": <subject of another version>}` succeeds (also `parent_topic_id` = itself). `add_catalogue_row` calls `check_topic_refs`, edit does not. Run `check_topic_refs` on the merged row for topics (also reject self/cyclic parent), and for strand (subject/level same version); add tests.
  2. **topic_progress is not repairable.** (a) Log s1 developing, s2 secure; then s2 is resubmitted with no topics: progress stays `secure`. (b) `rebuild_topic_progress` from the remaining evidence (s1 developing) also leaves `secure`, because `upsert_topic_progress` refuses evidence older than the stored row. A rebuild must overwrite unconditionally (separate `force`/replace path), set rows absent from the evidence (delete the stale row or reset), and the edit path (`submit_session_log` removing/lowering a topic of the LATEST log) must recompute from the child's remaining log entries instead of leaving stale mastery. Add tests for both, and make ties deterministic: `derive_progress` with two entries at the same `submitted_at` returns different results depending on input order (add a tiebreak, e.g. session_id).
  3. **L04 conflict:** #106 AC "Regenerated if older than the latest log", but `handover_summary` is immutable with unique `source_key = assignment:<id>`: a regeneration cannot be stored. Make the key carry a generation (or drop the unique in favour of (source, snapshot_at)), keep idempotent first generation, add a test that a second snapshot for the same assignment is accepted and a duplicate of the same generation is not.
  4. **Notes for decision (owner/L06, not blocking on their own):** #108 says Yes/Partly/No but `handover_rating.useful` is boolean (SR 5.3 says `useful`); consider a `rating` enum now (the table is immutable, a later change is a new column). #107 "read receipt stored" has no home (L05 adds a table). `level_equivalence` has no guarded write path in `catalogue_rules` (cross-version pairs and edits under a published version are not checked); `rebuild_topic_progress` takes log entries only, SR 5.3 says progress is rebuilt from logs AND assessments (document as follow-up). Edited `tests/test_schema.py` (one line: the duplicate-table test picked `domains[1]`, now looked up by name).
- **Decisions:** (1) Immutability: curators and clients have no write permission on any table (empty table permissions, validator-enforced); every catalogue write goes through the core-service rules above, which reject any edit/add/soft-delete under a published or retired version (`VersionImmutable`); only draft is editable; new wording = copy as draft, publish. The issue's "how we'll know" asks for this test, so the pure validator `assert_editable` is included. (2) `published_key` = curriculum id while published: at most one published version per curriculum; publishing retires the previous one, children keep their version so old versions keep resolving. (3) Soft-delete rule applied: every unique index on a soft table is over derived `*_key` columns (curriculum code, version, level/subject/topic codes, level pair, struggle label, one log per session, log topic/struggle, blueprint, item); `topic_progress` is `hard` (derived, rebuildable) so its `(child_id, topic_id)` unique is raw and literal as the AC says; `handover_summary`/`handover_rating` are `immutable` (unique `source_key` makes generation idempotent, unique (summary, teacher)). No new `SOFT_UNIQUE_LEGACY` entries. (4) Renames forced by the validator's money-word regex (`rate`): `handover_summary.generated_at` -> `snapshot_at`, `handover_rating.rated_by_teacher_id`/`rated_at` -> `teacher_id`/`created_at`. (5) `strand` added (not in SR 5.3 or the issue; live has it, `topic.strand_id` optional). (6) `struggle_tag` is not versioned (SR 5.3; live has `catalogue_version_id`) and is retired with `active=false`. (7) `topic` carries `catalogue_version_id` (SR lists subject_id, level_id; the issue requires version membership); same-version consistency of subject/level/strand/parent is a core rule (`check_topic_refs`). (8) Question: `options`/`answer_key` JSON text, `image_file_id`/`audio_file_id` Storage ids (non_fk), approver must differ from author (core rule + data validator, the schema cannot compare columns) and be a curator/senior teacher (core role check); approved questions are retired, not edited; `answer_key` never sent to the parent app before submit. ASM-7 marker not the child's teacher: `assert_marker_allowed`. (9) Log submit is idempotent per session; `topic_progress` update is a second write (no Appwrite transaction), repaired by `rebuild_topic_progress`; stale evidence (edit of an older session) never regresses newer progress. (10) Child data tables carry no table-level read; per-row grants are M05/M06 (note appended to #40).
